F400 · peripheral emulator

  • port_index_tableport_index_table — maps port number (000–3FF) to an offset into the NMI handler table.
  • INT0F_IRQ_DISPATCHINT0F_IRQ_DISPATCH — common hardware interrupt handler (vector INT 0Fh), replaces the 8259.
  • NMI_HANDLERNMI_HANDLER — NMI handler (vector INT 2): software emulation of IBM PC I/O ports.
  • nmi_port_handlersnmi_port_handlers — address table of emulated port handlers (42 words, index = byte/2
  • nmi_port_unusednmi_port_unused — port with no device: writes ignored, reads return FFh
  • nmi_port_004nmi_port_004 — 8237, channel 2 address (write-only). Bytes accumulate in 0040:009E/009F
  • nmi_port_005nmi_port_005 — 8237, channel 2 count (write-only). Bytes in 0040:00A2/00A3 via flip-flop 0040:00A4.
  • nmi_port_00Bnmi_port_00B — 8237, mode register (write-only): value stored at 0040:00B2.
  • nmi_port_040nmi_port_040 — 8253 channel 0 (system timer). The real 8253 at F000:0A00–0A06 is clocked at
  • nmi_port_041nmi_port_041 — 8253 channel 1 (RAM refresh on PC). Read — raw byte of real counter
  • nmi_port_042nmi_port_042 — 8253 channel 2 (speaker tone). Read — raw count byte F000:0A04.
  • nmi_port_043nmi_port_043 — 8253 control word: writes pass unchanged to the real 8253 (F000:0A06),
  • nmi_port_020nmi_port_020 — 8259 emulation: only non-specific EOI (20h) is recognized, which
  • nmi_port_060nmi_port_060 — 8255 port A (keyboard scan code) — mapped directly to the real 8255
  • nmi_port_061nmi_port_061 — 8255 port B (F000:0003). Bit 6 (keyboard clock hold) is inverted in "Praktik"
  • nmi_port_062nmi_port_062 — 8255 port C (F000:0005): direct mapping. Also holds common tail loc_06A8:
  • nmi_port_063nmi_port_063 — 8255 control register (F000:0007): direct mapping (POST writes 99h there,
  • nmi_port_081nmi_port_081 — DMA channel 2 page register (write-only): value (address bits 16–19)
  • nmi_port_201nmi_port_201 — game adapter (joystick) emulation. The real joystick is digital: with 8255 port B
  • nmi_port_378nmi_port_378 — LPT1 data register. Write — to printer output register F000:0A01 and copy
  • nmi_port_379nmi_port_379 — LPT1 status register (read-only). Bits 3 (ERROR), 5 (PE), 7 (BUSY, inverted)
  • nmi_port_37Anmi_port_37A — LPT1 control register. Write is stored at 0040:00BD and output to F000:0401
  • nmi_port_2F8nmi_port_2F8 — COM2 8250, RBR/THR register (DLL when DLAB=1) (receiver/transmitter or baud divisor low byte). Real UART mapped at F000:0DF0:
  • nmi_port_2F9nmi_port_2F9 — COM2 8250, IER register (DLM when DLAB=1) (interrupt enable or divisor high byte). Real UART mapped at F000:0DF2:
  • nmi_port_2FAnmi_port_2FA — COM2 8250, IIR register (interrupt identification). Real UART mapped at F000:0DF4:
  • nmi_port_2FBnmi_port_2FB — COM2 8250, LCR register (frame format, bit 7 DLAB). Real UART mapped at F000:0DF6:
  • nmi_port_2FCnmi_port_2FC — COM2 8250, MCR register (DTR/RTS/OUT1/OUT2/loopback). Real UART mapped at F000:0DF8:
  • nmi_port_2FDnmi_port_2FD — COM2 8250, LSR register (line status). Real UART mapped at F000:0DFA:
  • nmi_port_2FEnmi_port_2FE — COM2 8250, MSR register (modem status). Real UART mapped at F000:0DFC:
  • nmi_port_2FFnmi_port_2FF — COM2 8250, SCR register (scratch register). Real UART mapped at F000:0DFE:
  • nmi_port_3F8nmi_port_3F8 — COM1 8250, RBR/THR register (DLL when DLAB=1) (receiver/transmitter or baud divisor low byte). Real UART mapped at F000:0E10:
  • nmi_port_3F9nmi_port_3F9 — COM1 8250, IER register (DLM when DLAB=1) (interrupt enable or divisor high byte). Real UART mapped at F000:0E12:
  • nmi_port_3FAnmi_port_3FA — COM1 8250, IIR register (interrupt identification). Real UART mapped at F000:0E14:
  • nmi_port_3FBnmi_port_3FB — COM1 8250, LCR register (frame format, bit 7 DLAB). Real UART mapped at F000:0E16:
  • nmi_port_3FCnmi_port_3FC — COM1 8250, MCR register (DTR/RTS/OUT1/OUT2/loopback). Real UART mapped at F000:0E18:
  • nmi_port_3FDnmi_port_3FD — COM1 port 3FD: 8250 LSR (line status register).
  • nmi_port_3FEnmi_port_3FE — COM1 port 3FE: 8250 MSR (modem status). ZF=1 read, ZF=0 write
  • nmi_port_3FFnmi_port_3FF — COM1 port 3FF: 8250 SCR (scratch register). ZF=1 read, ZF=0 write
  • nmi_port_3D4nmi_port_3D4 — write to 6845 index register (CRTC address).
  • nmi_port_3D5nmi_port_3D5 — 6845 data register. Real 6845 is mapped: F000:0000 = address, F000:0002 = data.
  • nmi_port_3D8nmi_port_3D8 — write to CGA mode register (Mode Control).
  • nmi_port_3D9nmi_port_3D9 — write to CGA color select register (Color Select).
  • nmi_port_3DAnmi_port_3DA — read CGA status register (Status).
  • nmi_port_3F2nmi_port_3F2 — write to floppy drive control register (DOR, Digital Output Register).
  • nmi_port_3F4nmi_port_3F4 — read 765 main status register (MSR). Real 765: F000:0600 = MSR
  • nmi_port_3F5nmi_port_3F5 — 765 data register. Read — straight from F000:0602.
  • detect_com_portsdetect_com_ports — COM port detection at end of POST. Called from F000:E442 (call 4C04 via ROM mirror, phys. F4C04),
  • beepbeep — beep via 8253 timer channel 2 + port 61h (ports emulated via NMI).
  • font_hifont_hi — 8x8 font for characters 80h–FFh (alternative encoding, CP866), 8 bytes per character,

F000 · main BIOS

  • dat_E004dat_E004 — banner shown at startup (F000:E228 via sub_E6AE)
  • dat_E042dat_E042 — suffix for the tested-memory counter (printed at E34D)
  • dat_E051dat_E051 — option ROM checksum error message (sub_E46C, E473)
  • RESETRESET — reset entry point (jump from F000:FFF0, standard IBM address E05B).
  • spret_E06EReturn addresses for stackless subroutine calls (mov sp,addr + jmp trick)
  • spret_E070
  • sub_E447sub_E447 — RAM block test (DS=ES=segment, CX = length in words). Expects the block filled with FFh.
  • sub_E46Csub_E46C — option ROM checksum error message: «SSSS КС ПЗУ» ("SSSS ROM CS").
  • INT18_TAPE_LOADERINT18_TAPE_LOADER — cassette tape program loader monitor (INT 18h vector when BASIC is absent).
  • sub_E5DEsub_E5DE — keyboard line input with echo (program name for the tape loader).
  • sub_E62Fsub_E62F — checksum: sum of CX bytes at DS:BX (mod 256).
  • sub_E639sub_E639 — verify and initialize a found option ROM.
  • sub_E671sub_E671 — print address DS:DI as «SSSS:OOOO» (hex) via INT 10h/0Eh.
  • sub_E67Dsub_E67D — print AX as 4 hex digits (high byte first). In: AX. Clobbers CL.
  • sub_E684sub_E684 — print AL as two hex digits. In: AL. Clobbers CL, AH.
  • sub_E68Fsub_E68F — print one hex digit (AL = 0..0Fh) using the classic ADD 90h/DAA/ADC 40h/DAA trick.
  • sub_E695sub_E695 — print character AL via teletype (INT 10h, AH=0Eh, page 0).
  • sub_E69Csub_E69C — print a ROM message string with positioning.
  • sub_E6AEsub_E6AE — print ASCIIZ string from CS:SI via teletype from current cursor position.
  • INT19_BOOTINT 19h — bootstrap (standard IBM address F000:E6F2).
  • baud_tablebaud_table — 8250 divisors for INT 14h AH=0 (1.8432 MHz / 16 / divisor)
  • INT14_SERIALINT 14h — serial port (standard IBM address E739, code identical to IBM XT BIOS).
  • sub_E7F1sub_E7F1 — wait for bits BH to be set in port DX, with timeout.
  • sub_E809sub_E809 — advance pointer BX through the keyboard ring buffer (by a word) with wrap
  • INT16_KEYBOARDINT 16h — keyboard (standard IBM address E82E). AH=0 read with wait, 1 check, 2 flags.
  • dat_E85AINT 09h tables: scan codes of shift/toggle keys and their masks in flags 0040:0017/0018.
  • dat_E86ACtrl code table for scan codes 01h–3Ah (index = scan code−1); FFh = no code.
  • dat_E8A4Ctrl extended code table (AH) for scan codes 3Bh–53h (index = scan−3Bh): Ctrl-F1..F10 and Ctrl+keypad.
  • dat_E8D1Main ASCII table (unshifted, Latin) for scan codes 01h–3Ah (index = scan−1); FFh = no character.
  • INT09_KEYBOARD
  • dat_EA33Alt table: keypad digit scan codes (index = digit 0..9), then 26 letter scan codes
  • INT13_DISKETTEINT 13h — floppy disk service (standard IBM entry point F000:EC59).
  • sub_ECAFsub_ECAF — INT 13h function dispatcher.
  • sub_EE6Esub_EE6E — fetch floppy parameter table byte (INT 1Eh vector, 0000:0078).
  • sub_EE7Fsub_EE7F — send byte AH to 765 data register (3F5h).
  • sub_EEAAsub_EEAA — seek drive DL head to cylinder CH.
  • sub_EF12sub_EF12 — wait for floppy interrupt: poll flag 0040:003E bit 7 (set by INT 0Eh).
  • sub_EF36sub_EF36 — wait for IRQ + SENSE INTERRUPT STATUS (08h) and check ST0.
  • INT0E_DISKETTE_IRQINT 0Eh — floppy interrupt (standard entry point F000:EF57).
  • sub_EF65sub_EF65 — receive result bytes from floppy controller (NEC 765 result phase).
  • sub_EFAAsub_EFAA — count sectors actually transferred after an INT 13h operation.
  • diskette_paramsdiskette_params — floppy parameter table (INT 1Eh vector points here), 11 bytes as in IBM.
  • INT17_PRINTERINT17_PRINTER — printer service (standard IBM entry point F000:EFD2).
  • int10_func_tableint10_func_table — INT 10h function handler address table (AH=00h..0Fh), 16 words.
  • INT10_VIDEOINT10_VIDEO — BIOS video service (standard IBM entry point F000:F065).
  • sub_F092sub_F092 — set 6845 hardware cursor to position AX (AH=row, AL=column).
  • video_paramsvideo_params — 6845 video controller parameter table (INT 1Dh vector), 4 blocks of 16 bytes (R0–R15).
  • dat_F0E4dat_F0E4 — video page sizes in bytes indexed by (mode AND 0Eh) → 0040:004C (words).
  • dat_F0ECdat_F0EC — text column count for modes 0–7 → 0040:004A
  • dat_F0F4dat_F0F4 — CGA mode register values (port 3D8) for modes 0–7 → 0040:0065
  • int10_00_set_modeint10_00_set_mode — AH=00h, set video mode AL (0–7, no range check).
  • int10_04_read_lightpenint10_04_read_lightpen — AH=04h (light pen not supported) and also the common INT 10h epilogue.
  • int10_01_set_cursor_typeint10_01_set_cursor_type — AH=01h: CH = start, CL = end cursor scan line.
  • sub_F1CCsub_F1CC — write a 6845 register pair: AH → CH, AH+1 → CL.
  • int10_02_set_cursor_posint10_02_set_cursor_pos — AH=02h: BH = page, DH/DL = row/column.
  • int10_05_select_pageint10_05_select_page — AH=05h: AL = new active page.
  • int10_03_read_cursor_posint10_03_read_cursor_pos — AH=03h: BH = page.
  • int10_0B_set_paletteint10_0B_set_palette — AH=0Bh: BH=0 — background/border color BL (bits 0–4); BH=1 — palette BL bit 0.
  • int10_0F_get_modeint10_0F_get_mode — AH=0Fh: output AH = columns (0040:004A), AL = mode (0040:0049), BH = page (0040:0062).
  • sub_F264sub_F264 — byte offset of character cell within page: AX = (AH*columns + AL)*2.
  • int10_06_scroll_upint10_06_scroll_up — AH=06h: scroll window up.
  • sub_F2C1sub_F2C1 — text scroll setup.
  • sub_F2E8sub_F2E8 — copy one window row: DL words DS:SI → ES:DI (direction per DF). SI, DI preserved.
  • sub_F2F1sub_F2F1 — fill one window row: DL words of AX (char+attribute) at ES:DI. DI preserved.
  • int10_07_scroll_downint10_07_scroll_down — AH=07h: scroll window down (parameters as for AH=06h).
  • int10_08_read_char_attrint10_08_read_char_attr — AH=08h: BH = page. Output: AL = character, AH = attribute at cursor.
  • sub_F34Csub_F34C — character cell address at page BH cursor: BX = BH*page_size + position offset.
  • int10_09_write_char_attrint10_09_write_char_attr — AH=09h: AL = character, BL = attribute, BH = page, CX = repeat count.
  • int10_0A_write_charINT 10h, AH=0Ah — write character at cursor position without changing the attribute.
  • int10_0D_read_pixelINT 10h, AH=0Dh — read pixel (CGA graphics modes 4–6).
  • int10_0C_write_pixelINT 10h, AH=0Ch — write pixel (CGA graphics modes 4–6).
  • sub_F3D6sub_F3D6 — compute pixel address in CGA video memory (rows interleaved across banks 0000h/2000h).
  • sub_F4D2sub_F4D2 — copy one window line in both CGA banks.
  • sub_F4EBsub_F4EB — fill one window line with byte AL in both CGA banks.
  • sub_F631sub_F631 — replicate color to all pixels of a byte (320x200 mode).
  • sub_F646sub_F646 — "stretch" a glyph byte: each bit of AL is doubled (bit k → bits 2k and 2k+1).
  • sub_F668sub_F668 — pack 8 pixels of 2 bits (2 video memory bytes) into an 8-bit row image.
  • sub_F685sub_F685 / sub_F688 — character cell offset in graphics video memory (bank 0).

  • sub_FA34sub_FA34 — write one bit to tape. In: CF = bit.

  • sub_FA58sub_FA58 — update CRC-CCITT (polynomial 1021h) at 0040:0069 with one bit.
  • font_lofont_lo — 8×8 font for characters 00h–7Fh at the standard IBM address F000:FA6E (8 bytes per character, top to bottom,
  • INT1A_TIMEINT1A_TIME — INT 1Ah (IBM entry point F000:FE6E): system tick-count clock.
  • dat_FEA1dat_FEA1 — stub handler "mov ah,1 / iret": POST (F000:E192) fills all interrupt vectors with it

  • sub_FEE9sub_FEE9 — DS = 0040h (BIOS data area segment). AX preserved.

  • dat_FF03dat_FF03 — handler offsets for INT 10h..1Fh (segment F000), copied in POST (F000:E1BA).

Praktik PC BIOS, version 2.16-17

Complete disassembly with a comment on every line. The C.S.T. version (1992), reworked from the 1990 Magnit factory BIOS. Cassette loader on INT 18h.

Important. The Praktik motherboard power connector looks like the standard IBM PC 5.25-inch floppy drive power connector, BUT(!) its pinout does not match the standard one. Connecting a standard IBM PC power supply burns out the chips on the motherboard. Check the pinout before applying power.
3550 instructions4106 listing linesCPU 8088/8086"probably" = inferred from code

How this BIOS works

The Praktik lacks some of the standard IBM PC chips: there is no 8259 interrupt controller (Soviet КР580ВН59) and no 8237 DMA controller (КР1810ВТ37). The other chips — 8255 (КР580ВВ55А), 8253 (КР580ВИ53), 6845 (КМ1809ВГ6), 765 (КР1810ВГ72А) and two 8250s (no direct Soviet equivalent) — are present, but they sit in a memory window at F000:0000–0FFF instead of on the standard I/O ports. When a program accesses a standard IBM PC port, the hardware traps the access and raises an NMI. The NMI handler in ROM works out which port was accessed and performs the access in software: it forwards it to the real chip, converts the values, or emulates the device entirely. That is why ordinary IBM PC software runs even though the hardware is different.

The BIOS consists of two 8 KB halves:

  • First half (segment F400) is the peripheral emulator: the NMI handler with its port table, the common hardware interrupt handler INT 0Fh, the port handlers, programmed-I/O floppy data transfer, and the upper half of the font (characters 80–FFh).
  • Second half (F000:E000–FFFF) is an ordinary BIOS with entry points at exactly the IBM PC/XT addresses: POST, INT 09h–1Ah, bootstrap, and the font for characters 00–7Fh.

The CPU sees the ROM twice: at FC000–FFFFF and in a mirror at F4000–F7FFF. Through the mirror the main BIOS calls code in the first half with near calls (for example call beep at F000:4C2D). The INT 1Fh vector F000:5C00 also points through the mirror to the upper half of the font.

Hardware register map (F000:0000–0FFF)

AddressDevicePurpose
F000:0000 / 0002CRTC 6845Index and data registers. INT 10h AH=0 writes to them directly.
F000:0001 / 0003 / 0005 / 00078255Port A (scan code), port B (like 61h, bit 6 inverted), port C (tape, OUT2), control word (99h).
F000:0009—Read before INT 08h. Probably clears the timer interrupt request.
F000:0200NMI latch / IRQ requests; video modeRead — bits 0–9: port number; bit 10: 1 = OUT; bit 11: NMI caused by a port access; bits 12–15: IRQ from COM1, keyboard, timer, floppy. A word write sets the video mode: low byte — color (like 3D9), high byte — mode (like 3D8).
F000:0400 / 0401OUT dataThe byte written by the trapped OUT: 0400 for an even port, 0401 for an odd one. Reading 0400 acknowledges the NMI.
F000:0600 / 0601 / 0602FDC 7650600 — MSR, 0602 — data. 0601 — DOR equivalent (copy in 0040:00B9): bits 0–1 drive select, bit 2 /RESET, bit 3 IRQ enable, bits 4–7 motors.
F000:0800 / 0808FloppyDACK cycle for transferring one byte to/from the 765 without DMA; 0808 — the same with TC (last byte). The CPU executes WAIT before each byte: DRQ is probably wired to the TEST pin.
F000:0A00–0A06 (even)8253Channels 0–2 and the control word. Clocked at about 1 MHz, so the emulator rescales the divisors.
F000:0A01LPTWrite — printer data, read — status lines.
F000:0DF0–0DFE8250 COM2Registers 2F8–2FF, stride 2.
F000:0E10–0E1E8250 COM1Registers 3F8–3FF, stride 2.

Ports trapped by the NMI

PortsWhat is emulated
004, 005, 00B, 0818237 DMA, channel 2. Address, count, mode and page are stored in the BIOS data area 0040:009E–00B3 and used by the programmed-I/O floppy transfer.
0208259. Only EOI is recognized; it clears bit 5 of 8255 port B.
040–0438253, with divisors rescaled for the ~1 MHz clock.
060–0638255, forwarded directly (bit 6 of port 61h inverted).
201Game port. An analog joystick is simulated from a digital one.
2F8–2FF, 3F8–3FFCOM2 and COM1, forwarded to the real 8250s.
378–37ALPT1.
3D4, 3D5, 3D8, 3D9, 3DACGA. Writes to 6845 registers are filtered; the 3D8 mode register reloads R0–R9 from the emulator's own tables.
3F2, 3F4, 3F5Floppy. Sector data are transferred by the CPU, without DMA.
all others"No device" handler: OUT is ignored, IN returns FFh.

Differences from the IBM PC/XT

  • Interrupts. Instead of an 8259 there is a single request line and the INT 0Fh vector. The handler at F400:0400 reads bits 12–15 of F000:0200 and calls INT 0Eh, 08h, 09h or 0Ch. The INT 08h and 09h handlers send no EOI.
  • Keyboard. Bit 2 of 0040:0018 is the RUS/LAT switch (code E0 9Dh, right Ctrl release). There are ЙЦУКЕН (Russian JCUKEN) tables in CP866 and CapsLock for Cyrillic.
  • Floppy drives. The drives are 80-track. Bits 4–5 of 0040:003E mark a 720 KB disk. For 360 KB disks double stepping is used. INT 19h sets the flag from the BPB of the boot sector.
  • Video. There is no monochrome mode: video memory is always at B800 and the CRTC always at 3D4. The 6845 timing registers (R0–R8) are protected from program writes. Writing R9=1 in 80×25 mode enables a 160×100 graphics mode. The 3DA status is fake: the retrace bits toggle on every read.
  • Font. Characters 80–FFh are CP866, but F2h–FBh are replaced: rounded frame corners, arrows, ÷ and ±.
  • INT 18h. Instead of ROM BASIC there is a cassette program loader: enter a name, then search for the header on tape. INT 15h follows the IBM PC cassette algorithm.
  • POST. The memory test prints «NNN Кб проверено» ("NNN KB tested"). Error messages are in Russian. The checksum covers the 8 KB main ROM.

How to read the listing

Each line shows the address (segment:offset), the bytes, the instruction and a comment. Procedures are preceded by headers describing their purpose and input/output registers. Labels sub_XXXX are subroutines, loc_XXXX jump targets, dat_XXXX data referenced by code. Next to each label are the addresses that reference it. "Probably" marks a conclusion drawn from the code that is not confirmed by documentation. On-screen messages are quoted in the original Russian with an English translation.

Segment F400 — peripheral emulator (first 8 KB of ROM)

port_index_table — maps port number (000–3FF) to an offset into the NMI handler table.
Index = bits 0–9 of latch F000:0200; byte = 2*handler number in nmi_port_handlers (F400:049F).
Value 52h (handler #41 nmi_port_unused) — port not emulated: writes ignored, reads return FFh.
NMI_HANDLER reads word cs:[bx] from here and clears the high byte, i.e. only this byte is used.
port_index_table:
F400:000052db 4 dup (52h)Ports 000–003 (DMA 8237: address/count, channels 0–1) — not emulated
F400:00040Adb 0AhPort 004 (8237: channel 2 address) → nmi_port_004, handler #5
F400:00050Cdb 0ChPort 005 (8237: channel 2 count) → nmi_port_005, handler #6

INT0F_IRQ_DISPATCH — common hardware interrupt handler (vector INT 0Fh), replaces the 8259.
Reads the request register F000:0200 (high byte): bit 15 — floppy (INT 0Eh), bit 14 — timer (INT 08h),
bit 13 — keyboard (INT 09h), bit 12 — COM1 (INT 0Ch). One source is serviced per entry, by priority.
Special case: floppy IRQ during programmed FDC data transfer (0040:00B9 bit 3 = 0) — the stack
is cut back to the return address into NMI_HANDLER, i.e. the transfer loop in nmi_port_3F5 is aborted.
INT0F_IRQ_DISPATCH:

NMI_HANDLER — NMI handler (vector INT 2): software emulation of IBM PC I/O ports.
Hardware traps IN/OUT and raises NMI. F000:0200 — latch: bits 0–9 = port number,
bit 10 = 1 on write (OUT), 0 on read (IN); bit 11 = NMI caused by port access.
F000:0400/0401 — OUT data (even/odd bus byte). Handler is called with ZF=1 on IN,
ZF=0 on OUT, AX = latch, ES = F000, BP → frame: [bp]=program's AX (IN result goes here).
NMI_HANDLER:
F400:046C06push esSave ES (frame: [bp+6])
F400:046D55push bpSave BP ([bp+4])
F400:046E53push bxSave BX ([bp+2])
F400:046F50push axSave interrupted program's AX ([bp+0] — handler writes IN result here)
F400:04708BECmov bp, spBP → saved register frame
F400:0472B800F0mov ax, 0F000hHardware register segment F000h…
F400:04758EC0mov es, ax…into ES (ES stays F000 in all port handlers)
F400:0477268B1E0002mov bx, word es:[200h]BX = latch F000:0200: port number and access flags
F400:047CF6C708test bh, 8Bit 11: NMI caused by I/O port trap?
F400:047F7415je loc_0496No (other NMI source) — just acknowledge and exit
F400:04818BC3mov ax, bxAX = copy of latch (handlers test bits 10/11 in AH)
F400:048381E3FF03and bx, 3FFhKeep bits 0–9 — port number 000–3FF
F400:04872E8B1Fmov bx, word cs:[bx]Fetch byte from port_index_table (F400:0000+port)
F400:048A32FFxor bh, bhHigh byte unused: BX = offset into handler table (2*N)
F400:048C2E8B9F9F04mov bx, word cs:[bx + 49Fh]BX = port handler address from nmi_port_handlers
F400:0491F6C404test ah, 4Bit 10 (write): ZF=1 — IN read, ZF=0 — OUT write; flag passed to handler
F400:0494FFD3call bxCall emulated port handler
loc_0496: ← 047F
F400:049626A10004mov ax, word es:[400h]Read F000:0400 — probably acknowledges NMI/releases trap latch
F400:049A58pop axRestore AX (on IN — already holding result in AL)
F400:049B5Bpop bxRestore BX

F400:049D07pop esRestore ES

nmi_port_040 — 8253 channel 0 (system timer). The real 8253 at F000:0A00–0A06 is clocked at
~1 MHz (probably) rather than 1.19318 MHz, so values are rescaled: read ×1.19318 (98BAh/8000h),
write ×0.83809 (D68Dh/10000h). Low/high byte flip-flop — 0040:00A6, word buffer — 0040:00A8/00A9.

nmi_port_041 — 8253 channel 1 (RAM refresh on PC). Read — raw byte of real counter
F000:0A02 (no rescaling or flip-flop). Write — divisor accumulates in 0040:00AC/00AD via flip-flop 0040:00AA,
then N*8068/10000+1 (≈0.807*N) — probably a tuned factor for different channel 1 clocking.
Also holds common tail loc_05C5: AL → IN result and exit (used by channels 0–2).

nmi_port_201 — game adapter (joystick) emulation. The real joystick is digital: with 8255 port B
bit 7 = 1, port A (F000:0001) returns directions (bits 0–3, active low) and buttons (bits 4–7).
OUT 201h fires the "one-shots": counters stored at 0040:008C–008F (5 — min, 0Ah — center, 0Fh — max);
IN 201h decrements them, and an axis bit stays 1 until its counter reaches zero (mimics RC timing).

nmi_port_3D8 — write to CGA mode register (Mode Control).
The new value (with bit 3 forced on — video enabled) is stored at 0040:00B5.
When the mode type (bits 0–1) changes, the real 6845 is reprogrammed from table 08FD/090D/091D/092D;
then the pair (color 3D9, mode 3D8) is written to hardware register F000:0200/0201.

nmi_port_3DA — read CGA status register (Status).
There is no real retrace signal: on every read bits 0 (display enable/horizontal retrace) and
3 (vertical retrace) are toggled so programs waiting for retrace do not hang.
nmi_port_3DA: ← 04A7

765 floppy command emulation tables (used by nmi_port_3F5).
0A1C: 15 765 command codes (low 5 bits of first byte); 0A2B: 15 matching descriptor bytes:

bit6 = direction memory→floppy (write), bit5 = command raises no interrupt.

nmi_port_3F2 — write to floppy drive control register (DOR, Digital Output Register).
Drive select bits 0–1 are converted to a one-hot code (drive 0 → bit0, 1 → bit1), bit 3
(IRQ/DMA enable) is forced on; result goes to 0040:00B9 and hardware register F000:0601.
On reset release (bit 2: 0→1) the post-reset 765 interrupt is emulated: wait for floppy IRQ and call INT 0Eh.
nmi_port_3F2: ← 04D9
F400:0A3A51push cxsave interrupted program's registers used below

F400:0A3C57push disave DI

F400:0A53743Cje loc_0A91no (stay in reset) → just update register

F400:0A6BC606BA0000mov byte [0BAh], 00040:00BA — 765 command intake state: 0 = waiting for first byte of a new command
Wait for a 765 interrupt (after reset or command) and deliver it via INT 0Eh.
Polls bit 15 of word F000:0200 (floppy IRQ request) about 2x65536 times; on timeout exits silently.

nmi_port_3F5 — 765 data register. Read — straight from F000:0602.
Write — command/parameter byte; command bytes are counted via 0040:00BA (tables 0A1C/0A2B).
On the last byte of a command with a data phase, software "DMA" runs (instead of the 8237): transfer
to/from the buffer at the address/count previously written to ports 004/005/081, synchronized with WAIT.

Channel 2 pseudo-DMA (8237 emulation): buffer address = page (0040:00B3, port 081) : address (0040:009E,
port 004), length = 0040:00A2 (port 005) + 1. Bytes move via F000:0800 (DACK cycle to 765),
the last one via F000:0808 (DACK + TC). Each byte waits for ready via WAIT (probably TEST pin ← 765 DRQ).

detect_com_ports — COM port detection at end of POST. Called from F000:E442 (call 4C04 via ROM mirror, phys. F4C04),
i.e. runs with CS=F000, offset 4C04. Entry: DS=0040h, BX=0 — COM address table 0040:0000, DI — segment for field 0011h.
IN 3FA/2FA are trapped by NMI and handled by the 8250 emulator.
For 3F8/2F8: if the top 5 IIR bits = 0, the port exists and its address goes into the table; port count → equipment.
detect_com_ports:

F400:0C09ECin al, dxIN → NMI → nmi_port_3FA (real 8250, F000:0E14); read 8250 interrupt identification register
F400:0C0AA8F8test al, 0F8ha genuine 8250 has IIR bits 3–7 = 0
F400:0C0C7508jne loc_0C16no → COM1 absent
F400:0C0EC7870000F803mov word [bx], 3F8hstore base address 3F8h in table
F400:0C1443inc bxnext entry
F400:0C1543inc bxBX += 2
loc_0C16: ← 0C0C

F400:0C19ECin al, dxread IIR
F400:0C1AA8F8test al, 0F8hcheck bits 3–7
F400:0C1C7508jne loc_0C26no → COM2 absent
F400:0C1EC7870000F802mov word [bx], 2F8hbase address 2F8h to table

beep — beep via 8253 timer channel 2 + port 61h (ports emulated via NMI).
Called via ROM mirror (phys. F4C2D) with CS=F000, offset 4C2D: from F000:F712 (BEL in INT 10h AH=0Eh teletype, BL=2)
and from F000:F723/F72E (POST beeps, sub_F717).
Entry: BL — duration (BL*65536 iterations). Frequency 1193180/0533h ≈ 896 Hz.

sub_E447 — RAM block test (DS=ES=segment, CX = length in words). Expects the block filled with FFh.
Pass 1 (forward): each byte must be FFh, 00h is written; pass 2 (backward): each byte 00h.
Out: ZF=1 — OK; ZF=0 — failure, AL = XOR (failing bit mask), DI = failing byte offset.
Also called before the stack is set up (via mov sp,E070h / jmp) — then RET takes the address from ROM.

INT18_TAPE_LOADER — cassette tape program loader monitor (INT 18h vector when BASIC is absent).
40×25 screen, name input (up to 8 chars) into 0050:0001; reads headers (256 bytes at 0050:0009) via INT 15h AH=2.
Header: +0 = A5h, +1..8 = name, +0Ah = length, +0Ch = load segment, +0Eh = offset, +10h = entry offset.
On name match — loads the body and jumps far to load_segment:entry_offset. Does not return.

In: DS = ROM segment (signature AA55h), BX = 0, DX = same segment.

INT 19h — bootstrap (standard IBM address F000:E6F2).
Up to 4 attempts: reset floppy (INT 13h/00) and read sector 0/0/1 of drive A: into 0000:7C00.
Failure → INT 18h (here — cassette tape loader). Points INT 1Eh vector to parameter table EFC7.
INT19_BOOT:

F000:E6FCC7067800C7EFmov word [78h], 0EFC7hINT 1Eh vector (0000:0078) ← offset EFC7 of floppy parameter table
F000:E7028C0E7A00mov word [7Ah], csINT 1Eh segment ← CS (F000)

baud_table — 8250 divisors for INT 14h AH=0 (1.8432 MHz / 16 / divisor)
Index — bits 7–5 of AL at port init; the word is written to DLL/DLM with DLAB=1.
baud_table: ← E77A
F000:E7291704dw 0417h110 baud: divisor 0417h (1047)
F000:E72B0003dw 0300h150 baud: divisor 0300h (768)
F000:E72D8001dw 0180h300 baud: divisor 0180h (384)
F000:E72FC000dw 00C0h600 baud: divisor 00C0h (192)
F000:E7316000dw 0060h1200 baud: divisor 0060h (96)
F000:E7333000dw 0030h2400 baud: divisor 0030h (48)
F000:E7351800dw 0018h4800 baud: divisor 0018h (24)
F000:E7370C00dw 000Ch9600 baud: divisor 000Ch (12)
INT 14h — serial port (standard IBM address E739, code identical to IBM XT BIOS).
In: AH = function (0 init, 1 send, 2 receive, 3 status), AL = parameters/character, DX = port number.
Base address taken from 0040:0000+2*DX; accesses to 8250 ports (3F8–3FF/2F8–2FF) are trapped by NMI and emulated.
Out: AH = line status (LSR), AL = MSR / received character; AH bit 7 = timeout.
INT14_SERIAL:
F000:E739FBstienable interrupts
F000:E73A1Epush dssave DS
F000:E73B52push dxsave DX (port number)
F000:E73C56push sisave SI
F000:E73D57push disave DI

INT 09h — keyboard handler (entered at standard E987 → jmp here).
Called from the common INT 0Fh handler on a status bit in F000:0200. Scan code is read from the emulated
port 60h, acknowledged by pulsing bit 7 of port 61h (as with the XT 8255 PPI). Beyond the standard IBM functions:
prefix E0h → bit 1 of 0040:0018; right Ctrl (E0 9D) toggles RUS/LAT (bit 2 of 0040:0018).

F000:EC4700db 18 dup (00h)zero padding up to standard INT 13h entry point (F000:EC59)
INT 13h — floppy disk service (standard IBM entry point F000:EC59).
Functions: 0 reset, 1 status, 2 read, 3 write, 4 verify, 5 format, 8 drive parameters.
Input: AH=function, AL=sector count, CH=cylinder, CL=sector, DH=head, DL=drive (0–1), ES:BX=buffer.
Output: AH=status (0040:0041), CF=1 on error, AL=sectors processed.
765 controller (ports 3F2/3F4/3F5) and 8237 DMA channel 2 (ports 04/05/0A/0B/0C/81) — emulated via NMI.
Frame: [bp]=DX,[bp+2]=BP,[bp+4]=DI,[bp+6]=SI,[bp+8]=DS,[bp+0Ah]=CX,[bp+0Ch]=BX.
INT13_DISKETTE:
F000:EC59FBstienable interrupts (floppy IRQ needed → INT 0Eh via common handler F400:0400)
F000:EC5A53push bxsave BX (may be returned modified — function 8)
F000:EC5B51push cxsave CX (function 8 returns cylinders/sectors in it)
F000:EC5C1Epush dssave DS
F000:EC5D56push sisave SI
F000:EC5E57push disave DI (function 8 returns parameter table offset)
F000:EC5F55push bpsave BP
F000:EC6052push dxsave DX ([bp+1] = head number)
F000:EC618BECmov bp, spBP — base of saved-register frame
F000:EC63E88312call sub_FEE9DS = 0040h (BIOS data area)
F000:EC66E84600call sub_ECAFexecute function AH (dispatcher)
F000:EC69BB0400mov bx, 4BX=4 → parameter table byte 2 (motor-off delay), not sent to 765
F000:EC6CE8FF01call sub_EE6EAH = motor run-on time in timer ticks
F000:EC6F88264000mov byte [40h], ah0040:0040 — motor-off countdown (decremented in INT 08)
F000:EC738A264100mov ah, byte [41h]AH = operation status 0040:0041
F000:EC7780FC01cmp ah, 1CF=1 if status is 0 …
F000:EC7AF5cmc… inverted: CF=1 on non-zero status (error)
F000:EC7B5Apop dxrestore DX
F000:EC7C5Dpop bprestore BP
F000:EC7D5Fpop direstore DI (for function 8 — already replaced in frame)
F000:EC7E5Epop sirestore SI
F000:EC7F1Fpop dsrestore DS
F000:EC8059pop cxrestore CX
F000:EC815Bpop bxrestore BX
F000:EC82CA0200retf 2return discarding old FLAGS — CF is passed to the caller
Function 08h — drive parameters (reduced implementation).
Type is given by bit (10h << DL) in 0040:003E (non-standard: 80-track disk/drive flag, 720K).
Output: CX = max cylinder/sectors, BL = type (1=360K, 3=720K), ES:DI = parameter table (vector 1Eh).
DH/DL (heads/drive count) are not filled in.
loc_EC85: ← ECCB
F000:EC851Epush dssave DS (=0040h)
F000:EC86B010mov al, 10h"80 tracks" bit mask for drive 0 …

Function 00h — reset 765 floppy controller via DOR (3F2h).
Keeps running motors, reset pulse (bit 2=0 → 1), waits for IRQ and Sense Interrupt,
then SPECIFY command (03h) with bytes 0–1 of the parameter table.

sub_EE7F — send byte AH to 765 data register (3F5h).
Waits in MSR (3F4h) for DIO=0 (bit 6) and RQM=1 (bit 7). Ports are emulated by the NMI handler (FDC 765).
Timeout: status 80h, drops its own return address and returns one level up with CF=1.

sub_EEAA — seek drive DL head to cylinder CH.
On first access (drive bit in 0040:003E = 0) — RECALIBRATE twice (probably to make sure
track 0 is reached on an 80-track drive: 765 issues at most 77 steps). Then SEEK.
For disks without the "80 tracks" flag (bits 4–5 of 0040:003E) the cylinder is doubled (double-step).
Output: CF=1 on error. Also called from POST (E3EE/E3F5).

sub_EF36 — wait for IRQ + SENSE INTERRUPT STATUS (08h) and check ST0.

INT 0Eh — floppy interrupt (standard entry point F000:EF57).
Called from common handler F400:0400 by a bit in F000:0200. Sets flag 0040:003E bit 7.
Unlike IBM, no EOI is sent to port 20h — the common handler probably does the acknowledge.
INT0E_DISKETTE_IRQ:

F000:EF6258pop axrestore AX
F000:EF631Fpop dsrestore DS
F000:EF64CFiretreturn from interrupt
sub_EF65 — receive result bytes from floppy controller (NEC 765 result phase).
Reads up to 7 bytes from data port 3F5 into 0040:0042–0048 (ST0,ST1,ST2,C,H,R,N), polling MSR (3F4).
Output: CF=0 — success; CF=1 — error, 0040:0041 gets bit 80h (timeout) or 20h (controller failure).
Preserves CX, DX, BX; DS=0040. Ports 3F4/3F5 are emulated via NMI (765 emulation).
Called after read/write operations (EE1F, EE64) and after Sense Interrupt Status (EF40).
sub_EF65: ← EE1F EE64 EF40

sub_EFAA — count sectors actually transferred after an INT 13h operation.
Input: CH = requested cylinder, CL = starting sector; DS=0040, result bytes at 0040:0042–0048.
If the controller stayed on the same cylinder: AL = R(next sector) − CL;
otherwise (ran past end of track): AL = EOT+1 − CL (EOT from INT 1Eh parameter table).
Output: AL = sector count; clobbers AH, BX.
sub_EFAA: ← EE60 EE68
F000:EFAAA04500mov al, byte [45h]AL = 0040:0045 — cylinder (C) from 765 result bytes
F000:EFAD3AC5cmp al, chmatches requested cylinder CH?
F000:EFAFA04700mov al, byte [47h]AL = 0040:0047 — sector number (R) where the controller stopped
F000:EFB2740Aje loc_EFBEsame cylinder — count from R
F000:EFB4BB0800mov bx, 8BX = 8 → floppy parameter table byte 4 (EOT, sectors per track), not sent to FDC
F000:EFB7E8B4FEcall sub_EE6EAH = parameter from INT 1Eh table (sub_EE6E)
F000:EFBA8AC4mov al, ahAL = last sector of track (EOT)
F000:EFBCFEC0inc alAL = EOT+1 — "next" after the last
loc_EFBE: ← EFB2
F000:EFBE2AC1sub al, clAL = next sector number − starting sector = sectors transferred
F000:EFC0C3retReturn
F000:EFC1000000000000db 00h,00h,00h,00h,00h,00hzero padding up to fixed table address F000:EFC7 (as in IBM PC/XT)

INT17_PRINTER — printer service (standard IBM entry point F000:EFD2).
Input: AH=0 — print character AL, AH=1 — initialize, AH=2 — read status; DX = printer number (0–2).
Output: AH = printer status (bits as in IBM: 80h not busy, 40h ACK, 20h out of paper, 10h selected, 08h error, 01h timeout).
Base port taken from 0040:0008+2*DX, timeout from 0040:0078+DX. Ports 378–37A are emulated via NMI (LPT emulation).

INT10_VIDEO — BIOS video service (standard IBM entry point F000:F065).
Saves ES,DS,DX,CX,BX,SI,DI; AX is popped before jumping to the handler.
On handler entry: DS=0040, ES=B800 (CGA video memory), AL = original AL, AH = current mode (0040:0049),
SI = 2*function. Functions ≥10h are ignored. All handlers exit via common epilogue F1B9.

video_params — 6845 video controller parameter table (INT 1Dh vector), 4 blocks of 16 bytes (R0–R15).

int10_00_set_mode — AH=00h, set video mode AL (0–7, no range check).
Disables video (3D8←0), programs the 16 6845 registers DIRECTLY via hardware registers F000:0000 (index)
and F000:0002 (data) from the INT 1Dh table, clears video memory B800, fills 0040:0049–0066,
enables mode (3D8) and palette (3D9). CRTC address is always 3D4 (no mono adapter).

int10_04_read_lightpen — AH=04h (light pen not supported) and also the common INT 10h epilogue.

In: DX=row (0–199), CX=column; AH=video mode. Out: AL=pixel color.

INT15_CASSETTE — INT 15h (IBM entry point F000:F859): cassette tape interface, algorithm as in the IBM PC.
AH=0/1 motor on/off (stubs here), 2 — read blocks into ES:BX (CX bytes),
3 — write blocks from ES:BX. Out: CF=1 error, AH = code (1 CRC, 2 data lost, 4 no data, 80h invalid function).
Interval measurement/generation via channel 2 of the real 8253 mapped at F000:0A00..0A06 (channel 2 = F000:0A04).
Exit via retf 2 discards the saved FLAGS to return the result CF.

sub_F99D — measure a half-period of the tape signal.
Waits for bit 4 of F000:0005 to change (cassette input, like bit 4 of IBM port 62h), then
latches channel 2 of the real 8253 (F000:0A06 ← 80h) and reads the count from F000:0A04.
Out: BX = elapsed time (previous - current count), 0040:0067 = current count; CX=0 — timeout.

sub_FA34 — write one bit to tape. In: CF = bit.
Period: 1 → 3E8h ticks, 0 → 1F4h. Waits for the current OUT2 period of channel 2 to end
(bit 5 of F000:0005, like bit 5 of IBM port 62h) and loads the new count into F000:0A04 (falls through to sub_FA4E).

AH=0: CX:DX = 0040:006E:006C, AL = midnight rollover flag 0040:0070 (cleared);
AH=1: set counter from CX:DX. Other functions ignored.

INT08_TIMER — INT 08h (IBM entry point F000:FEA5), called from the common handler F400:0400
on bit 14 of F000:0200. Tick counter 0040:006C/6E, reset after 24 hours (1800B0h ticks),
floppy motor-off timeout 0040:0040, call to user INT 1Ch.
No EOI is sent — there is no 8259.

loc_FF3D — end of DMA setup for INT 13h (continuation of sub_F73E, jumped to from F000:F770).

sub_FFCB — line feed on printer LPT1: outputs LF (0Ah), then CR (0Dh) via INT 17h
(CR is printed by the tail at F000:E97E, which does the ret). Clobbers AX, DX=0.
sub_FFCB: ← FF73 FFA5
F000:FFCB33D2xor dx, dxDX = 0 — printer LPT1

F000:FFD0CD17int 17hprint LF
F000:FFD2E9A9E9jmp loc_E97Etail at E97E: print CR (0Dh) and ret
sub_FFD5 — keyboard poll during tape operations.
Reads the scan code from F000:0001 (8255 port A, analog of 60h) and acknowledges the keyboard by pulsing
bits 7 and 5 in F000:0003 (8255 port B; bit 5 — clears keyboard interrupt request, cf. F400:043E).
Out: AL = scan code, ZF = 1 if Esc pressed (scan 01h).
sub_FFD5: ← F8BF F8E1 F91E
F000:FFD52EA00100mov al, byte cs:[1]AL = scan code from F000:0001 (8255 port A)
F000:FFD950push axsave scan code
F000:FFDA2EA00300mov al, byte cs:[3]AL = current value of F000:0003 (8255 port B)
F000:FFDE8AE0mov ah, alAH = original port B value
F000:FFE00CA0or al, 0A0hbits 7 (keyboard reset) and 5 (clear interrupt request) = 1
F000:FFE22EA20300mov byte cs:[3], alassert acknowledge pulse
F000:FFE62E88260300mov byte cs:[3], ahrestore original port B value
F000:FFEB58pop axAL = scan code
F000:FFEC3C01cmp al, 1Esc (scan 01h)? → ZF
F000:FFEEC3retreturn
F000:FFEF00db 00hpadding up to the reset vector F000:FFF0
POWER_ON — CPU reset vector (FFFF:0000 = F000:FFF0): jump to RESET F000:E05B.
Followed by the ROM version date and the model type byte.
POWER_ON:
F000:FFF0EA5BE000F0jmp far RESETfar jump to POST start F000:E05B
F000:FFF530312F3031…db '01/01/91'ROM date «01/01/91» (standard IBM location F000:FFF5)
F000:FFFD00FFCDdb 00h,0FFh,0CDh00h; FFh — model ID (as IBM PC); CDh — probably the checksum adjustment byte
